I don't really know about seeing things. But... sometimes I hear things.

I don't know. I guess it's changed. Like I said before... I just feel kind of flat. Even with stuff I used to like... music or whatever... I don't really feel excited about it anymore. I just don't feel much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that you think are true, but others might not believe?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. I mean... I know it probably sounds weird. But... at work, I do bookkeeping, and... sometimes I look at the numbers. Like invoice totals or account numbers. And... I just feel like they mean something else. Like they're messages about me. My supervisor tells me they're just normal entries, but... I don't know. I still feel like they're tracking me, trying to see if I figure out the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that the numbers in the invoices or account numbers are actually messages about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I really believe it. I know it sounds strange, but... I just get convinced that they're messages. Like the totals, or... even the due dates. My supervisor shows me they're just normal entries, but... I don't know. I still feel like they're tracking me... to see if I understand the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that the numbers in the invoices or account numbers are messages about you, even when your supervisor explains they are not?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I still do. I mean, he shows me the paperwork and explains it all, but... I just can't shake it. It still feels like they're messages. Like they're watching to see if I catch on.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situation or evidence that could make you change your mind about the numbers being messages about you?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... probably not. It's just... the totals, the account numbers, the due dates... they just feel like messages. Even when he shows me the regular entries, it doesn't change anything. I just... I still think they're tracking if I get the pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty understanding other people's feelings or points of view?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I guess I understand people fine. I don't really have a problem with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're pushing people away or is it more that you're just not drawn to social interactions anymore?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'm pushing them away, exactly. I still... care about them. It's just... I let texts sit there a lot now. I don't really go out with them after work anymore. Even when they talk about stuff we used to do, like going to shows or whatever... I just feel kind of flat about it. I don't really have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the belief that numbers are messages about you is something that might be false, or do you think it's definitely true?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I mean, I know it sounds strange to other people. But... I get so convinced that those invoice totals and due dates and everything... that they're messages about me. Like I said, even when my supervisor points out that they're just normal entries... it doesn't really matter. I still feel like they're tracking if I understand the pattern. So... I guess I think it's true.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the messages you believe are hidden in numbers at work are also hidden in other parts of your life, like in things you see or hear outside of work?\n\nPatient: I don't really know about seeing things. But... sometimes I hear things. \n\nLike, when I'm alone at my desk, or... walking home from class. I'll hear a man's voice. He just says things like... \"check line seven\" or \"you missed the sign.\" It sounds real. Clear enough that I usually look around to see who said it, but... nobody is ever there. \n\nI don't really like talking about that part, though.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for you to be wrong about the voices you hear and the messages in the numbers?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. Maybe. \n\nIt just feels really real when it happens. I don't really want to talk about it anymore.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone, do you find it hard to stay on topic or keep your thoughts in order?\n\nPatient: I guess sometimes. People say they can't follow me. Like... if I'm trying to explain why a ledger is off at work. I start talking about the numbers, but then... my mind just goes to my tuition deadlines. And then tax rules. And... studio lighting, and the patterns in the numbers. They tell me they don't get how I went from one thing to the next. I don't know. It makes sense to me when I'm saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to express your thoughts in a clear and coherent way to others?\n\nPatient: Yeah... like I just said. When I try to explain why a ledger is off, my thoughts just jump from tuition deadlines to tax rules... and then to studio lighting and the number patterns. \n\nPeople tell me they can't follow how I got from one point to the next. So... yeah, I guess it's difficult. I don't really have anything else to add to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your way of thinking has changed over time, becoming more disorganized or hard to follow for others?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know if it's changed. I mean, I just told you what happens. Like with the ledgers and the tuition, and the lighting... people say they can't follow it. I don't know if it's getting worse. It's just... how it is right now. I don't really know what else to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more jumbled or confusing now than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I... I feel like we just talked about this. I don't know if it's more jumbled now. It's just... like I said before. When I'm looking at a ledger that's off, I start talking about that, but then I'm talking about my tuition, and tax rules, and... studio lighting. And the patterns. People just say they can't follow me. I don't know if it's worse now. It's just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you are in control of your actions when you are doing things like lining up receipts or standing by the copier\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure about control. I guess I don't really think about it. It's just... things I do that don't make sense. That's actually why I came in. \n\nI've just started doing these things at work and school. Like lining the receipts all the way across the floor, or... tapping my calculator in sets of nine. And with the copier... I just stand there with my hands held up. I stay like that until someone asks if I'm okay. I don't really have a good reason for why I do it. It just happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
